===== .changeset/r17-security-at-the-gate.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/integrity": minor
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/adapters": minor
"@olympus-ai/readiness": minor
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/conformance": minor
"olympus-ai": minor
---

R17: security at the gate. `CheckKind` gains `secret-scan` and
`static-analysis`, each a `SecurityCheckSpec` that pins an image by digest
and its rules as text, and no command. Admission refuses a manifest that pins
no secret-scan check, or no static-analysis check over a stack with a
static-analysis slot, and a security check of any image but the one declared
for its kind. The runtime runs each over a copy of the tree with the
scanner's in-tree ignore files removed, its rules on a second read-only
mount, with no egress, at the base and at `verify`. `CheckResult` gains
`findings`, fingerprinted by the runtime independent of line (D-R17-02); only
a finding the base does not account for fails the check (`new-finding`), and
a suppression marker the diff adds fails it (`suppression-added`) and is
recorded as a `suppression-marker` violation (`TamperReport` gains
`suppressionMarkersAdded`). Every failed `verify` iteration, of any check, is
recorded as a `station-refused` decision with its `gate-failed` refusal, now
decided by the line (D-R17-06). The scanners are gitleaks 8.21.2, whose image
moves from `readiness` to `adapters` and is re-exported, and Semgrep 1.95.0,
the TypeScript set's new `staticAnalysis` slot; a set without one names
`static-analysis` among its missing controls.

Every spec carries a security part, `RequestedArtifacts.specParts.security`,
locked with the spec (`AdmissionRecord.artifacts.specParts`). A spec without
one is refused at `spec` (`spec-part-missing`), and an authorization rule it
names with no acceptance or behavioral check naming it in `deniesRule` refuses
the `test-design` exit (`denial-check-missing`). `BaseMismatch.declared`
admits `null` for a security check with no readable findings on the base. A
run admitted before this does not resume.

The conformance registry adds ten R17 assertions under I2, I3, and I5, two of
them over the real images (needs Docker), and the P14 table's two new rows; its `station:gate-failed` row, unconstructed
until now, is a scenario.

The CLI's `create` takes `--security-part <path>`, sent as
`artifacts.specParts.security`; the HTTP create body accepts it as its one
optional artifact key.

       };

       // The probe's whole report is the evidence; the expectation is the verdict drawn from it.

===== packages/adapters/src/security.ts =====
/**
 * The scanners a security check runs (R17). Each is declared once, here: the
 * image it is pinned to, the arguments the runtime runs it with, the inline
 * suppression markers its grammar has, the in-tree files it reads whatever
 * it is told, and how its report becomes findings. A manifest pins the image
 * and the rules; nothing in a manifest or a repository chooses the
 * arguments (D-R17-01).
 *
 * A finding's fingerprint is derived here from the report's fields, never
 * read from the scanner's own: gitleaks fingerprints by line, so a moved line
 * would read as a new finding (D-R17-02).
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { posix } from 'node:path';
import type { Finding, SecurityCheckKind } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';

export interface SecurityScanner {
  readonly kind: SecurityCheckKind;
  /** `name@sha256:<digest>`: the only image a check of this scanner's kind may pin. */
  readonly image: string;
  /**
   * Inline markers that suppress a finding in this scanner's grammar,
   * matched case-insensitively as substrings of a line the diff added. Each
   * is a failure of its own whatever the scanner does with it (D-R17-04).
   */
  readonly markers: readonly string[];
  /**
   * Files, by base name, the scanner reads from the tree it scans whatever
   * its arguments say. The runtime removes every one from the copy it scans
   * (D-R17-03).
   */
  readonly inTreeConfig: readonly string[];
  /**
   * The rules a check that pins `config: null` runs with, as the text of a
   * configuration file that defers to the image's own; `null` when the
   * scanner has no rules of its own, and a check of it must pin some.
   */
  readonly defaultConfig: string | null;
  /** The base name the rules are written under in the sandbox: each scanner reads its configuration's format from the extension. */
  readonly configFile: string;
  /** The argument vector, run from the root of the tree it scans, with its rules at `config` in the sandbox. */
  invocation(config: string): readonly [string, ...string[]];
  /** The exit codes of a scan that ran to its end, findings or none. Any other exit is not a report. */
  readonly completed: readonly number[];
  /** The findings `stdout` reports, or undefined when it is not a whole report: never an empty list for a report it could not read (I5). */
  parse(stdout: string): Finding[] | undefined;
}

/** gitleaks 8.21.2, pinned by digest. R1's secret probe runs the same image (D-R17-01). */
export const SECRET_SCAN_IMAGE = 'zricethezav/gitleaks@sha256:0e99e8821643ea5b235718642b93bb32486af9c8162c8b8731f7cbdc951a7f46';

/** Semgrep 1.95.0, pinned by digest. It runs the rules a check pins and fetches none: `verify` has no egress. */
export const STATIC_ANALYSIS_IMAGE = 'semgrep/semgrep@sha256:30e6afa99ebd8e7b4115d4904898108eb4bf77025819e9263f09cf14e6f6e549';

function sha256(text: string): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(text).digest('hex');
}

/** A report path as the tree names it: forward slashes, no leading `./`, no sandbox mount prefix. */
export function treeRelative(path: string): string {
  let p = path.replace(/\\/g, '/');
  if (p.startsWith('/workspace/')) p = p.slice('/workspace/'.length);
  while (p.startsWith('./')) p = p.slice(2);
  return p;
}

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function parseJson(text: string): unknown {
  try {
    return JSON.parse(text) as unknown;
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}

/**
 * A secret's fingerprint: its rule and its value, and not its path or line,
 * so the same credential is the same finding wherever it moves (D-R17-02).
 * Only the hash leaves this function; the value is never stored.
 */
export function secretFingerprint(rule: string, secret: string): string {
  return sha256(`secret-scan\0${rule}\0${secret}`);
}

/** A static-analysis finding's fingerprint: its rule, its file, and the code it matched with whitespace collapsed, and not its line. */
export function staticFingerprint(rule: string, path: string, lines: string): string {
  return sha256(`static-analysis\0${rule}\0${path}\0${lines.trim().replace(/\s+/g, ' ')}`);
}

/** gitleaks' JSON report: an array of findings, each with `RuleID`, `File`, `StartLine`, and the unredacted `Secret`. */
export function parseGitleaksReport(stdout: string): Finding[] | undefined {
  const report = parseJson(stdout);
  if (!Array.isArray(report)) return undefined;
  const findings: Finding[] = [];
  for (const entry of report) {
    if (!isRecord(entry)) return undefined;
    const { RuleID: rule, File: file, StartLine: line, Secret: secret } = entry;
    if (typeof rule !== 'string' || typeof file !== 'string' || typeof line !== 'number' || typeof secret !== 'string') return undefined;
    // A redacted report has nothing to fingerprint, and every secret in it would collide.
    if (secret === '' || secret === 'REDACTED') return undefined;
    findings.push({ rule, location: { path: treeRelative(file), line }, fingerprint: secretFingerprint(rule, secret) });
  }
  return findings;
}

/**
 * Semgrep's JSON report. A scan that reports an error skipped what the error
 * names, so it is not a whole report and none of it is read (I5).
 */
export function parseSemgrepReport(stdout: string): Finding[] | undefined {
  const report = parseJson(stdout);
  if (!isRecord(report) || !Array.isArray(report.results) || !Array.isArray(report.errors)) return undefined;
  if (report.errors.length > 0) return undefined;
  const findings: Finding[] = [];
  for (const entry of report.results) {
    if (!isRecord(entry) || !isRecord(entry.start) || !isRecord(entry.extra)) return undefined;
    const { check_id: rule, path } = entry;
    const { line } = entry.start;
    const { lines } = entry.extra;
    if (typeof rule !== 'string' || typeof path !== 'string' || typeof line !== 'number' || typeof lines !== 'string') return undefined;
    // Semgrep withholds the matched code from a scan with no login; such a report cannot be fingerprinted.
    if (lines === 'requires login') return undefined;
    const relative = treeRelative(path);
    findings.push({ rule, location: { path: relative, line }, fingerprint: staticFingerprint(rule, relative, lines) });
  }
  return findings;
}

/**
 * gitleaks over the tree. `--config` always names the runtime's file, since
 * with none gitleaks reads `.gitleaks.toml` from the tree; it reads
 * `.gitleaksignore` from the tree whatever `--gitleaks-ignore-path` says, so
 * the runtime scans a copy without one (D-R17-03). `--ignore-gitleaks-allow`
 * reports a suppressed secret, and `--exit-code 0` makes a finding a report
 * rather than an error. No `--redact`: the fingerprint needs the value, and
 * the runtime never stores the stdout that carries it (D-R17-02).
 */
export const SECRET_SCANNER: SecurityScanner = {
  kind: 'secret-scan',
  image: SECRET_SCAN_IMAGE,
  markers: ['gitleaks:allow'],
  inTreeConfig: ['.gitleaksignore', '.gitleaks.toml'],
  defaultConfig: '[extend]\nuseDefault = true\n',
  configFile: 'gitleaks.toml',
  invocation: (config) => [
    'gitleaks', 'dir', '.', '--no-banner', '--log-level', 'error',
    '--config', config, '--gitleaks-ignore-path', posix.dirname(config), '--ignore-gitleaks-allow',
    '--report-format', 'json', '--report-path', '/dev/stdout', '--exit-code', '0',
  ],
  completed: [0],
  parse: parseGitleaksReport,
};

/**
 * Semgrep over the tree, with the pinned rules and nothing fetched.
 * `--disable-nosem` reports a suppressed finding, and `--no-git-ignore`
 * scans what `.gitignore` would hide; `.semgrepignore` is read from the tree
 * whatever it is told, so the runtime scans a copy without one (D-R17-03).
 */
export const STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER: SecurityScanner = {
  kind: 'static-analysis',
  image: STATIC_ANALYSIS_IMAGE,
  // `nosem` is a prefix of `nosemgrep`, so one substring covers both of Semgrep's spellings.
  markers: ['nosem'],
  inTreeConfig: ['.semgrepignore'],
  defaultConfig: null,
  configFile: 'rules.yaml',
  invocation: (config) => [
    'semgrep', 'scan', '--config', config, '--json', '--metrics=off', '--disable-version-check',
    '--disable-nosem', '--no-git-ignore', '--quiet', '.',
  ],
  completed: [0],
  parse: parseSemgrepReport,
};

/** Each declared marker on a line, matched case-insensitively. */
export function markersIn(line: string, markers: readonly string[]): string[] {
  const lower = line.toLowerCase();
  return markers.filter((marker) => lower.includes(marker.toLowerCase()));
}

/**
 * The fingerprints `after` has more of than `base` does, one entry per
 * occurrence over the base's count. Counted rather than set-compared, so a
 * finding the base had once and the change copies elsewhere is new
 * (D-A-SC-02).
 */
export function newFindings(base: readonly Finding[], after: readonly Finding[]): Finding[] {
  const remaining = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const f of base) remaining.set(f.fingerprint, (remaining.get(f.fingerprint) ?? 0) + 1);
  const added: Finding[] = [];
  for (const f of after) {
    const left = remaining.get(f.fingerprint) ?? 0;
    if (left > 0) remaining.set(f.fingerprint, left - 1);
    else added.push(f);
  }
  return added;
}

===== packages/adapters/src/stand-in.ts =====
/**
 * Stand-in scanners: the `SecurityScanner` contract over a sandbox that runs
 * no image. Each is a `node` script run in the tree it scans, so the line's
 * security checks run on the stub sandbox, which spawns commands on the
 * host. They find only what a fixture seeds, and the graph builder declares
 * any scanner it does not attest unsafe, so no run on one goes above L1
 * (R17). They report in the format of the scanner they stand in for, and
 * are parsed by its parser, so the runtime reads them as it reads the real
 * reports.
 */
import { parseGitleaksReport, parseSemgrepReport, SECRET_SCANNER, type SecurityScanner } from './security.js';

/** A secret no real rule set matches and a seeded fixture can spell: `SEEDED-SECRET-` and twenty capitals or digits. */
export const SEEDED_SECRET = /SEEDED-SECRET-[A-Z0-9]{20}/;

/** Code the stand-in static-analysis scanner reports: a call to `eval`. */
export const SEEDED_SINK = /\beval\(/;

/** Not an image: a name no registry serves, at a digest of zeros, so a stand-in can never be mistaken for a pin. */
export const STAND_IN_SECRET_SCAN_IMAGE = `stand-in/secret-scan@sha256:${'0'.repeat(64)}`;
export const STAND_IN_STATIC_ANALYSIS_IMAGE = `stand-in/static-analysis@sha256:${'0'.repeat(64)}`;

/**
 * Walks the working directory, skipping `node_modules` and VCS directories,
 * and prints each match of `pattern` on any line as `report` renders it. It
 * honours a `.gitleaksignore` in the tree it scans, as gitleaks does, so a
 * runtime that failed to remove one would be caught by the stand-in too.
 */
function script(pattern: RegExp, report: 'gitleaks' | 'semgrep'): string {
  return [
    "const fs = require('node:fs'); const path = require('node:path');",
    `const pattern = new RegExp(${JSON.stringify(pattern.source)}, 'g');`,
    "const skip = new Set(['node_modules', '.git', '.hg', '.sl', '.svn']);",
    "const ignored = new Set(fs.existsSync('.gitleaksignore') ? fs.readFileSync('.gitleaksignore', 'utf8').split(/\\r?\\n/).filter(Boolean) : []);",
    'const found = [];',
    'const walk = (dir) => {',
    '  for (const entry of fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {',
    '    const file = path.join(dir, entry.name);',
    '    if (entry.isDirectory()) { if (!skip.has(entry.name)) walk(file); continue; }',
    '    if (!entry.isFile()) continue;',
    "    const rel = path.relative('.', file).split(path.sep).join('/');",
    "    fs.readFileSync(file, 'latin1').split(/\\r?\\n/).forEach((text, i) => {",
    '      for (const m of text.matchAll(pattern)) found.push({ rel, line: i + 1, text, match: m[0] });',
    '    });',
    '  }',
    '};',
    "walk('.');",
    report === 'gitleaks'
      ? "process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(found.filter((f) => !ignored.has(`${f.rel}:seeded-secret:${f.line}`)).map((f) => ({ RuleID: 'seeded-secret', File: f.rel, StartLine: f.line, Secret: f.match }))));"
      : "process.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({ results: found.map((f) => ({ check_id: 'seeded-sink', path: f.rel, start: { line: f.line }, extra: { lines: f.text } })), errors: [] }));",
  ].join('\n');
}

/** Stands in for gitleaks: the same markers, in-tree files, and report format, over `SEEDED_SECRET`. */
export const STAND_IN_SECRET_SCANNER: SecurityScanner = {
  kind: 'secret-scan',
  image: STAND_IN_SECRET_SCAN_IMAGE,
  markers: SECRET_SCANNER.markers,
  inTreeConfig: SECRET_SCANNER.inTreeConfig,
  defaultConfig: SECRET_SCANNER.defaultConfig,
  configFile: SECRET_SCANNER.configFile,
  invocation: () => ['node', '-e', script(SEEDED_SECRET, 'gitleaks')],
  completed: [0],
  parse: parseGitleaksReport,
};

/** Stands in for Semgrep: its markers and report format, over `SEEDED_SINK`. Its rules are pinned, and it reads none of them. */
export const STAND_IN_STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER: SecurityScanner = {
  kind: 'static-analysis',
  image: STAND_IN_STATIC_ANALYSIS_IMAGE,
  markers: ['nosem'],
  inTreeConfig: ['.semgrepignore'],
  defaultConfig: null,
  configFile: 'rules.yaml',
  invocation: () => ['node', '-e', script(SEEDED_SINK, 'semgrep')],
  completed: [0],
  parse: parseSemgrepReport,
};

===== packages/adapters/test/security.test.ts =====
import { describe, expect, test } from 'vitest';
import { markersIn, newFindings, parseGitleaksReport, parseSemgrepReport, staticFingerprint, treeRelative } from '../src/index.js';

describe('security scanner reports (R17)', () => {
  test('a gitleaks report becomes findings with the runtime\'s fingerprint, and a redacted or malformed one is no report', () => {
    const report = JSON.stringify([{ RuleID: 'github-pat', File: '/workspace/src/a.ts', StartLine: 3, Secret: 'ghp_x' }]);
    const [finding] = parseGitleaksReport(report) ?? [];
    expect(finding).toMatchObject({ rule: 'github-pat', location: { path: 'src/a.ts', line: 3 } });
    expect(finding?.fingerprint).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
    expect(JSON.stringify(finding)).not.toContain('ghp_x');
    expect(parseGitleaksReport(JSON.stringify([{ RuleID: 'r', File: 'a', StartLine: 1, Secret: 'REDACTED' }]))).toBeUndefined();
    expect(parseGitleaksReport('not json')).toBeUndefined();
    expect(parseGitleaksReport('[]')).toEqual([]);
  });

  test('a semgrep report with an error, or with its matched code withheld, is no report', () => {
    const result = { check_id: 'rules.no-eval', path: './x.ts', start: { line: 2 }, extra: { lines: '  eval(x);' } };
    expect(parseSemgrepReport(JSON.stringify({ results: [result], errors: [] }))).toEqual([
      { rule: 'rules.no-eval', location: { path: 'x.ts', line: 2 }, fingerprint: staticFingerprint('rules.no-eval', 'x.ts', 'eval(x);') },
    ]);
    expect(parseSemgrepReport(JSON.stringify({ results: [result], errors: [{ message: 'parse error' }] }))).toBeUndefined();
    expect(parseSemgrepReport(JSON.stringify({ results: [{ ...result, extra: { lines: 'requires login' } }], errors: [] }))).toBeUndefined();
  });

  test('findings are compared by count, so a copy of a base finding is new and a move is not', () => {
    const f = (fingerprint: string, line: number) => ({ rule: 'r', location: { path: 'a', line }, fingerprint });
    expect(newFindings([f('x', 1)], [f('x', 9)])).toEqual([]);
    expect(newFindings([f('x', 1)], [f('x', 1), f('x', 2)])).toEqual([f('x', 2)]);
    expect(newFindings([], [f('y', 1)])).toEqual([f('y', 1)]);
  });

  test('markers match case-insensitively, and a report path is made tree-relative', () => {
    expect(markersIn('const a = 1; // NOSEMGREP', ['nosem'])).toEqual(['nosem']);
    expect(markersIn('const a = 1;', ['gitleaks:allow'])).toEqual([]);
    expect(treeRelative('.\\src\\a.ts')).toBe('src/a.ts');
    expect(treeRelative('/workspace/b.ts')).toBe('b.ts');
  });
});

===== packages/api/src/base.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/api/src/base.ts b/packages/api/src/base.ts
index 7cdc427..39e4450 100644
--- a/packages/api/src/base.ts
+++ b/packages/api/src/base.ts
@@ -18,50 +18,59 @@ export function declaredChecks(checks: readonly CheckSpec[]): DeclaredCheckSpec[
 /**
  * Each declared check that did not do on the base what it declared. A check
  * with no result is `no-result`, never a failure: a test that never ran did
  * not fail (I5). One that ran and did the opposite of its declaration is
  * `contrary`. A suite check, or one that pins `expectedSuiteCount`, that did
  * what it declared over suites that could not be counted, or counted below
  * what it requires, is `suite-count`. An acceptance check's count is how many
  * of its named tests the runner it was run with selects, so one below the
  * number named is a test the runner never loaded, and its failure
  * reproduces nothing (D-R15-04). This is `verify`'s gate rule. A
  * behavioral check that pins no count runs the product rather than a runner,
  * so its exit code alone decides.
  */
 export function baseMismatches(checks: readonly CheckSpec[], record: BaseRunRecord): BaseMismatch[] {
   const mismatched: BaseMismatch[] = [];
   for (const check of declaredChecks(checks)) {
     const result = record.checks.find((r) => r.checkId === check.id);
     if (result === undefined) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: null, cause: 'no-result' });
       continue;
     }
     const failed = result.exitCode !== 0;
     if (failed !== (check.onBase === 'fails')) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'contrary' });
       continue;
     }
     const counted = result.suiteCount;
     const required = requiredSuites(check);
     const unknown = counted === null && (SUITE_KINDS.has(check.kind) || required !== undefined);
     if (unknown || (counted !== null && required !== undefined && counted < required)) {
       mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: check.onBase, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'suite-count' });
     }
   }
+  // A security check declares nothing; it runs over the base for its findings, and one with none
+  // readable there leaves no base set to judge a finding by, so the exit is refused (D-R17-01).
+  for (const check of checks) {
+    if (check.kind !== 'secret-scan' && check.kind !== 'static-analysis') continue;
+    const result = record.checks.find((r) => r.checkId === check.id);
+    if ((result?.findings ?? null) === null) {
+      mismatched.push({ checkId: check.id, declared: null, exitCode: result?.exitCode ?? null, cause: 'no-result' });
+    }
+  }
   return mismatched;
 }

     baseRun: ref,

 }

 /** The runtime's own stations: lock what was admitted for them, then the station is exiting. */
 async function work(ctx: LineContext, station: StationId): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {

+      // A part every spec carries is refused before anything is locked: the run stops here (R17).
+      const missing = specPartRefusal(ctx.securityPart);
+      if (missing !== undefined) return missing;
+      const refusal = await lockAdmitted(ctx, 'spec', specArtifacts(ctx));
       if (refusal !== undefined) return refusal;
       break;
     }
     case 'test-design': {
+      // Read from what was admitted, so it is refused before anything is locked or run (R17).
+      const undenied = denialRefusal(ctx.securityPart, ctx.checks);
+      if (undenied !== undefined) return undenied;

         attempts[task.id] = { iterations: 0, retries: 0, starts: 0 };
       }

     case 'integrate':

     case 'review':
     case 'observe':
     case 'learn':

 /**
  * Locks a station's admitted artifacts and holds the lock to admission: an

 /**
  * Runs every check that declares what it does on the base over a fresh copy of it, each in a fresh
  * sandbox, read-only and with no egress, exactly as `verify` runs a check over
  * base and the diff, and writes what they did to the Vault once (D-A-BF-01).
  * No driver is invoked. A check that does not start is recorded as unstarted
  * with its reason, and no exit code is invented for it (A-P6-02).
  */
 async function runOnBase(ctx: LineContext): Promise<VaultRef | StationRefusal> {
   const { sandbox, vault, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const tree = baseRunPath(workspaces, ctx.run.id);
   await copyTree(basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id), tree);
   const before = await tamperedIn(ctx, tree);
   if (before.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'test-design', null, before, { phase: 'base' });
   const runner = await suiteRunner(basePath(workspaces, ctx.run.id));
 
   const checks: CheckResult[] = [];
   const unstarted: UnstartedCheck[] = [];
   for (const check of declaredChecks(ctx.checks)) {
     const missing = await runnerMissing(check, runner, tree);
     if (missing !== undefined) {
       unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: missing });
       continue;
     }
     let handle: SandboxHandle;
     try {
       handle = await sandbox.provision(checkSandbox(ctx, tree, check));
     } catch (error) {
       unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: `its sandbox could not be provisioned: ${describe(error)}` });
       continue;
     }
     try {
       const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
       try {
         // The pinned argument vector, or a suite check's runner, exactly: no shell, no splitting (A-P6-01, D-R15-04).
         const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...commandOf(check, runner)]);
         checks.push({
           checkId: check.id,
           exitCode: exec.exitCode,
           stdout: exec.stdout,
           stderr: exec.stderr,
           suiteCount: await suitesRun(check, runner, tree),
           expectation: null,
+          findings: null,
           durationMs: exec.durationMs,
           startedAt,
         });
       } catch (error) {
         unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: describe(error) });
       }
     } finally {
       const { egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle);
       await recordEgress({ vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: null, station: 'test-design' }, egress);
     }
   }
+  // Each security check runs over the base for its findings, which are the base set every finding at
+  // `verify` is judged against (D-A-SC-02). One that produces none refuses the exit (D-R17-01).
+  for (const [index, check] of ctx.checks.entries()) {
+    if (!isSecurityCheck(check)) continue;
+    const scanned = await runSecurity(ctx, tree, 'base', index, check, null);
+    if (scanned.ok) checks.push(scanned.result);
+    else unstarted.push({ checkId: check.id, reason: scanned.reason });
+  }
 
   // Results collected over a changed artifact are not results, whatever the provider claims about ro.
   const after = [...(await tamperedPaths(ctx)), ...(await tamperedIn(ctx, tree))];
   if (after.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'test-design', null, after, { phase: 'after-base', checks });
   return vault.recordBaseRun({ runId: ctx.run.id, baseTreeSha256: ctx.baseTreeSha256, checks, unstarted, collectedBy: 'runtime' });
 }
 
+/**
+ * Runs one security check over `tree` through `scanTree` (R17), and records
+ * what its sandbox's proxy decided, whatever the scan did (D-P14-04).
+ */
+async function runSecurity(
+  ctx: LineContext, tree: string, label: string, index: number, check: SecurityCheckSpec, taskId: TaskId | null,
+): Promise<{ ok: true; result: CheckResult } | { ok: false; at: 'provision' | 'exec'; reason: string }> {
+  const { sandbox, vault, workspaces, profile } = ctx.components;
+  const scanned = await scanTree({
+    sandbox,
+    tree,
+    scratch: scanPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, label, index),
+    check,
+    scanner: scannerFor(check.kind, ctx.components.scanners),
+    limits: profile === null ? null : profile.limits,
+    user: workspaces.user,
+  });
+  if (scanned.ok || scanned.at === 'exec') {
+    await recordEgress({ vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId, station: ctx.state.station }, scanned.egress);
+  }
+  return scanned.ok ? { ok: true, result: scanned.result } : { ok: false, at: scanned.at, reason: scanned.reason };
+}

 async function build(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { driver, vault, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS.build, driver.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
   const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   // The task has not run, so the mismatch is not its role's doing.

   // Read before the iteration is spent: context that changed under the check costs no budget.
   if (drifted.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'build', task, drifted, { phase: 'context' }, null);

   if (started !== undefined) return started;

   // exactly base and what was accepted, never what an earlier attempt left behind.

   const base = await accepted(ctx);

   await copyTree(start, work);
   const offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>> = {
     'locked-spec': spec.text,
     'acceptance-tests': tests.text,
     'task-graph': JSON.stringify(ctx.graph.tasks.map(({ id, station, role, dependsOn }) => ({ id, station, role, dependsOn }))),
   };

   // among them, is a driver speaking outside the contract; it is not recorded, and the run stops.
   const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);
   if (problems.length > 0) {
     throw new Error(`line: the driver's result for task ${task.id} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);
   }
   const ref = await vault.recordTaskResult(ctx.run.id, ran.result);
   await commit(ctx, { tasks: { ...ctx.state.tasks, [task.id]: 'verifying' }, results: { ...ctx.state.results, [task.id]: ref } });

  * check ids, exit codes, whether an expectation held, the checks that never

 /**
  * `review`: one seat. The authors' families come from their recorded results;
  * the seat is checked before the reviewer runs, from the model it resolves, and
  * again after, from the model its result reports, so a driver that runs another
  * model than it resolved is held to the one that ran (I6). The stricter seat is
  * the one committed.
  */
 async function review(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { reviewer, vault } = ctx.components;
   const contract = STATION_CONTRACTS.review;
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(contract, reviewer.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
   const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   // The reviewer has not run; the mismatch is not the review seat's doing.
   if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'review', task, tampered, { phase: 'before-review' }, null);
 
   const reviewed = ctx.graph.tasks.filter((t) => task.dependsOn.includes(t.id));
   const authored = await Promise.all(reviewed.map((t) => readResult(ctx, t)));
   const authors = await authorsOf(ctx, reviewed, authored);
   const level = ctx.run.requestedLevel;
   const planned = seatReviewer(task.id, authors, reviewer.resolveModel(tierFor(scopeFor(ctx, task), 'review', attemptsOf(ctx.state, task))), level);
   if (!planned.ok) return planned;
 
-  const spec = await readText(ctx, ctx.artifacts.spec);
+  const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
   const tests = await readText(ctx, ctx.artifacts.acceptanceTests);
   const drifted = [...spec.tampered, ...tests.tampered];
   if (drifted.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'review', task, drifted, { phase: 'context' }, null);
 
   const started = await startAttempt(ctx, task);
   if (started !== undefined) return started;
   // Everything the line has is offered, the author's narrative and the plan
   // included; grantedContext is what keeps a seat to its contract.
   // The seat's tree holds the files the diff left, and a removal leaves none; the listing is how
   // the seat sees one. It is the runtime's diff, never the claim's file list (codex-5).
   const current = await accepted(ctx);
   const offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>> = {
     'locked-spec': spec.text,
     'acceptance-tests': tests.text,
     diff: JSON.stringify(current.diff.map(({ path, change }) => ({ path, change }))),
     'evidence-bundle': await evidenceFacts(ctx, task.dependsOn),
     'author-narrative': authored.map((r) => r.claim.narrative).join('\n'),
     plan: JSON.stringify(ctx.graph.tasks),
   };
   const ran = await runTask(ctx, task, reviewer, joinParts(grantedContext(contract, offered)), await reviewView(ctx, task));
   if (!ran.ok) return spendRetry(ctx, task);
   // I2: the line receives a result here too, and holds it to the contract exactly as build does (codex-6).
   const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);
   if (problems.length > 0) {
     throw new Error(`line: the reviewer's result for task ${task.id} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);
   }
   const seated = seatReviewer(task.id, authors, ran.result.model, level);
   if (!seated.ok) return seated;
   // The stricter of the two seats: what the reviewer reports never widens what the runtime resolved (I2, I6; codex-1, gemini-1).
   const seat = planned.seat.independence === 'reduced' ? planned.seat : seated.seat;
   const ref = await vault.recordTaskResult(ctx.run.id, ran.result);
   await commit(ctx, {
     tasks: { ...ctx.state.tasks, [task.id]: 'passed' },
     results: { ...ctx.state.results, [task.id]: ref },
     review: seat,
   });
   return undefined;
 }

  * The admission record, read back and checked in full: a record that does not
  * hold what the line reads is refused, not trusted (D-P1-12).

 async function readAdmission(vault: Vault, state: RunState): Promise<{ record: AdmissionRecord; policy: Policy }> {
   const parsed = parseJson(await vault.read(state.admission));
   const r = (typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed !== null ? parsed : {}) as Partial<Record<keyof AdmissionRecord, unknown>>;
   const run = r.run as Partial<Run> | undefined;
   const artifacts = r.artifacts as Partial<AdmissionRecord['artifacts']> | undefined;

     typeof run === 'object' && run.id === state.runId && typeof run.repo === 'string' && typeof run.baseCommit === 'string' &&

   const lost = await lostReadings(vault, state);
   if (lost.length > 0) {
     await resumeRefused({ reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: lost });
     return { ok: false, reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: lost, state };
   }
   // Not recorded: admission refuses this record before any state exists, so no run reaches a resume with it.
   const controls = admissionRefusal(record);
   if (controls !== undefined) return controls;
   const capability = capabilityRefusalFor(req.components);
   if (capability !== undefined) {
     await resumeRefused({ reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, refusal: capability.refusal });
     return { ok: false, reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, transition: capability.refusal, state };
   }
   // The base every workspace is built from must be the one admission recorded. A store that lost
   // it, or holds another run's, is refused rather than built on.
   const base = await treeDigest(basePath(req.components.workspaces, req.runId)).catch(() => 'missing');
   if (base !== record.baseTreeSha256) {
     throw new Error(`run ${req.runId}: the workspace store's base is ${base}, not the ${record.baseTreeSha256} admission recorded; refusing to resume on it`);
   }
 
   const reloaded = await reloadExecuted(record);
   const ctx: LineContext = {
     run: record.run,
     policy,
     artifacts: record.artifacts,
     graph: reloaded.graph,
     checks: reloaded.checks,
+    securityPart: reloaded.securityPart,
     components: req.components,
     baseTreeSha256: record.baseTreeSha256,
     state,
     ...hooks,
   };
   if (reloaded.changed.length > 0) return runLine(ctx, reloaded.changed);
   return runLine(ctx);
 }
 
 /**
  * Records a human's approval of the exit the run is waiting at, and nothing
  * else. The key must be exactly the station the run is exiting and the level
  * it was admitted at, and the effective approval there must be
  * `human-required`: a `blocked` exit cannot be approved, an `auto` one needs no
  * approval, and a grant for any other station or level would be a standing
  * approval of something nobody has seen yet. A grant already recorded and not
  * yet spent is not duplicated; one already spent does not stand in for the
  * visit now waiting, so a rebuild is approved in its own right (A-P4-04).
  */

===== packages/api/src/scan.ts =====
/**
 * One security check's run over one tree (R17), apart from the line so the
 * conformance suite can run it over a real daemon with the real scanners.
 * The line runs every security check through it, at the base and at
 * `verify`, and records the egress it returns.
 */
import { mkdir, readdir, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { SecurityScanner } from '@olympus-ai/adapters';
import type { CheckResult, SecurityCheckSpec } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import type { EgressLog, SandboxHandle, SandboxProvider, SandboxSpec } from '@olympus-ai/sandbox';
import { renderFindings } from './security.js';
import { copyTree } from './workspace.js';

/** Where a security check's rules are mounted in its sandbox, beside the tree at `/workspace` and outside it. */
export const SCAN_CONFIG = '/scanner-rules';

export interface ScanOptions {
  readonly sandbox: SandboxProvider;
  /** The tree the check judges. It is copied, never mounted. */
  readonly tree: string;
  /** A directory the runtime owns and nothing else uses: the copy and the rules are written under it, and it is removed when the scan ends. */
  readonly scratch: { readonly dir: string; readonly tree: string; readonly config: string };
  readonly check: SecurityCheckSpec;
  readonly scanner: SecurityScanner;
  /** The host profile's limits, or `null` on a graph with no profile: a sandbox that runs no image, which applies none. */
  readonly limits: Readonly<Omit<SandboxSpec['limits'], 'wallClockMs'>> | null;
  readonly user: SandboxSpec['user'];
}

export type ScanOutcome =
  | { readonly ok: true; readonly result: CheckResult; readonly egress: EgressLog }
  | { readonly ok: false; readonly at: 'provision'; readonly reason: string }
  | { readonly ok: false; readonly at: 'exec'; readonly reason: string; readonly egress: EgressLog };

function describe(error: unknown): string {
  if (error instanceof Error) return error.message;
  return typeof error === 'string' ? error : 'unknown error';
}

/** Removes every entry under `root` whose base name is one of `names`, at any depth; a link is removed, never followed. */
async function removeNamed(root: string, names: readonly string[]): Promise<void> {
  if (names.length === 0) return;
  const wanted = new Set(names);
  const visit = async (dir: string): Promise<void> => {
    for (const entry of await readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true })) {
      const path = join(dir, entry.name);
      if (wanted.has(entry.name)) await rm(path, { recursive: true, force: true });
      else if (entry.isDirectory()) await visit(path);
    }
  };
  await visit(root);
}

/**
 * Runs the check's scanner over a copy of `tree` with every file the scanner
 * reads from a tree whatever it is told removed (D-R17-03), with the locked
 * rules on a second read-only mount, in the image the check pins, with no
 * egress. The arguments are the scanner declaration's, never the
 * manifest's. Its stdout carries what it found, a secret's value included,
 * so the result keeps the runtime's rendering of the findings in its place
 * and the scanner's own output is never returned (D-R17-02). An exit outside
 * the scanner's completed codes, or a report it cannot parse, is findings
 * `null`: never an empty list (I5).
 */
export async function scanTree(options: ScanOptions): Promise<ScanOutcome> {
  const { sandbox, scratch, check, scanner } = options;
  const rules = check.config ?? scanner.defaultConfig;
  // Admission refuses a check with no rules for a scanner that has none of its own.
  if (rules === null) throw new Error(`scan: ${check.kind} check ${check.id} pins no rules, and its scanner has none of its own`);
  await rm(scratch.dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  await mkdir(scratch.config, { recursive: true });
  try {
    await copyTree(options.tree, scratch.tree);
    await removeNamed(scratch.tree, scanner.inTreeConfig);
    await writeFile(join(scratch.config, scanner.configFile), rules);
    const spec: SandboxSpec = {
      // The image is the check's pin, never the host's profile; a sandbox that runs no image ignores it.
      image: check.image,
      mounts: { workspace: { source: scratch.tree, target: '/workspace', mode: 'ro' }, others: [{ source: scratch.config, target: SCAN_CONFIG, mode: 'ro' }] },
      egress: { mode: 'deny-all', allow: [] },
      limits: options.limits === null ? { cpus: 0, memoryMb: 0, pids: 0, wallClockMs: check.timeoutMs } : { ...options.limits, wallClockMs: check.timeoutMs },
      user: { ...options.user },
    };
    let handle: SandboxHandle;
    try {
      handle = await sandbox.provision(spec);
    } catch (error) {
      return { ok: false, at: 'provision', reason: `its sandbox could not be provisioned: ${describe(error)}` };
    }
    let outcome: { ok: true; result: CheckResult } | { ok: false; reason: string };
    let egress: EgressLog;
    try {
      const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
      try {
        // A scanner writes caches under its home; the container's own is not the runtime's to assume writable.
        const exec = await sandbox.exec(handle, [...scanner.invocation(`${SCAN_CONFIG}/${scanner.configFile}`)], { env: { HOME: '/tmp' } });
        const findings = scanner.completed.includes(exec.exitCode) ? scanner.parse(exec.stdout) ?? null : null;
        outcome = {
          ok: true,
          result: {
            checkId: check.id,
            exitCode: exec.exitCode,
            stdout: renderFindings(findings),
            stderr: exec.stderr,
            suiteCount: null,
            expectation: null,
            findings,
            durationMs: exec.durationMs,
            startedAt,
          },
        };
      } catch (error) {
        outcome = { ok: false, reason: describe(error) };
      }
    } finally {
      // Read after the scan whatever it did, so the caller records it (D-P14-04).
      ({ egress } = await sandbox.destroy(handle));
    }
    return outcome.ok ? { ok: true, result: outcome.result, egress } : { ok: false, at: 'exec', reason: outcome.reason, egress };
  } finally {
    await rm(scratch.dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
}

===== packages/api/src/security.ts =====
/**
 * Security at the gate (R17): the spec's security part, the scanners a graph
 * runs, and the rules that turn a security check's result, the base's
 * findings, and the diff's suppression markers into a gate outcome, in the
 * runtime's terms alone (I2). Each rule is exported so it is assertable
 * without a run.
 */
import {
  newFindings,
  SECRET_SCANNER,
  STAND_IN_SECRET_SCANNER,
  STAND_IN_STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER,
  STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER,
  type SecurityScanner,
} from '@olympus-ai/adapters';
import type { FailedCheck, StationRefusal } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { CheckResult, CheckSpec, Finding, SecurityCheckKind, SecurityCheckSpec, TamperReport } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import type { RequestProblem } from './validate.js';

/** The scanners a graph runs a security check with, by kind. */
export interface SecurityScanners {
  readonly secretScan: SecurityScanner;
  /** The static-analysis scanner a stack with one is given; the adapter set decides whether the stack has one. */
  readonly staticAnalysis: SecurityScanner;
}

/** The scanners the graph builder attests. Any other is a stand-in, and no run on it goes above L1. */
export const SCANNERS: SecurityScanners = Object.freeze({ secretScan: SECRET_SCANNER, staticAnalysis: STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER });

/** The stand-in scanners, for a graph over a sandbox that runs no image. The graph builder declares them unsafe, so no run on them goes above L1. */
export const STAND_IN_SCANNERS: SecurityScanners = Object.freeze({ secretScan: STAND_IN_SECRET_SCANNER, staticAnalysis: STAND_IN_STATIC_ANALYSIS_SCANNER });

export function isSecurityCheck(check: CheckSpec): check is SecurityCheckSpec {
  return check.kind === 'secret-scan' || check.kind === 'static-analysis';
}

/** The scanner a security check runs: the one its kind is declared for. Admission refused any other image. */
export function scannerFor(kind: SecurityCheckKind, scanners: SecurityScanners): SecurityScanner {
  return kind === 'secret-scan' ? scanners.secretScan : scanners.staticAnalysis;
}

/** An authorization rule a change adds or relies on, by the id a denial check names. */
export interface AuthorizationRule {
  readonly id: string;
  readonly rule: string;
}

/**
 * What a spec says about its security surface (R17). `none` with one line
 * of reason is a valid answer, read by the person at the `spec` exit; any
 * other answer names every field, each list possibly empty.
 */
export type SecurityPart =
  | { readonly none: string }
  | {
    readonly trustBoundaries: readonly string[];
    readonly sensitiveData: readonly string[];
    readonly authorizationRules: readonly AuthorizationRule[];
    readonly secrets: readonly string[];
  };

const PART_LISTS = ['trustBoundaries', 'sensitiveData', 'secrets'] as const;

function isNonEmptyString(value: unknown): value is string {
  return typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '';
}

/** The security part, or the problems that keep it from being one. */
export function parseSecurityPart(value: unknown): { ok: true; part: SecurityPart } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
  const at = 'artifacts.specParts.security';
  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
  const report = (path: string, message: string): void => {
    problems.push({ path, code: 'not-a-security-part', message });
  };
  if (typeof value !== 'object' || value === null || Array.isArray(value)) {
    report(at, `${at} must be a JSON object: { "none": reason } or every field the part requires`);
    return { ok: false, problems };
  }
  const v = value as Record<string, unknown>;
  if ('none' in v) {
    if (!isNonEmptyString(v.none)) report(`${at}.none`, `${at}.none must say in one line why the change has no security surface`);
    const others = Object.keys(v).filter((key) => key !== 'none');
    if (others.length > 0) report(at, `${at} answers none and also names ${others.join(', ')}; it is one answer or the other`);
    return problems.length > 0 ? { ok: false, problems } : { ok: true, part: { none: v.none as string } };
  }
  for (const list of PART_LISTS) {
    const entries = v[list];
    if (!Array.isArray(entries) || !entries.every(isNonEmptyString)) report(`${at}.${list}`, `${at}.${list} must be a list of non-empty strings, empty if there are none`);
  }
  const rules = v.authorizationRules;
  const seen = new Set<string>();
  if (!Array.isArray(rules)) report(`${at}.authorizationRules`, `${at}.authorizationRules must be a list of { id, rule }, empty if there are none`);
  else {
    rules.forEach((rule: unknown, i) => {
      const one = `${at}.authorizationRules[${String(i)}]`;
      const r = (typeof rule === 'object' && rule !== null ? rule : {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
      if (!isNonEmptyString(r.id) || !isNonEmptyString(r.rule)) report(one, `${one} must name an id and the rule it states`);
      else if (seen.has(r.id)) report(`${one}.id`, `${one}.id '${r.id}' is listed twice; a denial check names exactly one rule`);
      else seen.add(r.id);
    });
  }
  if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
  return {
    ok: true,
    part: {
      trustBoundaries: [...(v.trustBoundaries as string[])],
      sensitiveData: [...(v.sensitiveData as string[])],
      authorizationRules: (rules as AuthorizationRule[]).map((r) => ({ id: r.id, rule: r.rule })),
      secrets: [...(v.secrets as string[])],
    },
  };
}

/** The refusal of the `spec` exit for a spec admitted without its security part. */
export function specPartRefusal(part: SecurityPart | null): StationRefusal | undefined {
  if (part !== null) return undefined;
  return {
    ok: false,
    reason: 'spec-part-missing',
    part: 'security',
    message: 'the spec carries no security part; every spec states the trust boundaries, data, authorization rules, and secrets it touches, or none with its reason',
  };
}

/**
 * The refusal of the `test-design` exit for each authorization rule the
 * security part names that no locked acceptance or behavioral check names
 * as the rule it denies.
 */
export function denialRefusal(part: SecurityPart | null, checks: readonly CheckSpec[]): StationRefusal | undefined {
  if (part === null || 'none' in part) return undefined;
  const denied = new Set(checks.flatMap((c) => (c.kind === 'acceptance' || c.kind === 'behavioral') && c.deniesRule !== undefined ? [c.deniesRule] : []));
  const [first, ...rest] = part.authorizationRules.map((r) => r.id).filter((id) => !denied.has(id));
  if (first === undefined) return undefined;
  const rules: [string, ...string[]] = [first, ...rest];
  return {
    ok: false,
    reason: 'denial-check-missing',
    rules,
    message: `no locked check exercises a caller refused by ${rules.join(', ')}; each authorization rule the security part names needs a check that names it in deniesRule`,
  };
}

/**
 * Why a security check fails the gate, or undefined when it does not. A
 * marker the diff added fails it whatever the scanner found, since a
 * suppressed finding is never a passing one. A report that could not be read
 * is no result, and an exit outside the scanner's completed codes is an
 * error, never a clean scan (I5). Otherwise only a finding the base's result
 * does not account for fails it (D-A-SC-02).
 */
export function securityShortfall(
  check: SecurityCheckSpec,
  result: CheckResult | undefined,
  base: readonly Finding[],
  scanner: SecurityScanner,
  tamper: Pick<TamperReport, 'suppressionMarkersAdded'>,
): FailedCheck | undefined {
  if (tamper.suppressionMarkersAdded.some((m) => m.kind === check.kind)) {
    return { checkId: check.id, exitCode: result?.exitCode ?? null, cause: 'suppression-added' };
  }
  if (result === undefined) return { checkId: check.id, exitCode: null, cause: 'no-result' };
  if (!scanner.completed.includes(result.exitCode)) return { checkId: check.id, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'exit-code' };
  if (result.findings === null) return { checkId: check.id, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'no-result' };
  if (newFindings(base, result.findings).length > 0) return { checkId: check.id, exitCode: result.exitCode, cause: 'new-finding' };
  return undefined;
}

/**
 * What a security check's stored stdout is: the runtime's rendering of its
 * findings, never the scanner's own output, which carries a secret's value
 * (D-R17-02).
 */
export function renderFindings(findings: readonly Finding[] | null): string {
  if (findings === null) return 'the scanner wrote no report the runtime could read\n';
  return `${JSON.stringify(findings.map((f) => ({ rule: f.rule, path: f.location.path, line: f.location.line, fingerprint: f.fingerprint })), null, 2)}\n`;
}

 export async function analyzeTamper(base: string, head: string, options: TamperOptions): Promise<TamperReport> {

   }

+
+/** A regular file's text, or the empty string where the tree has none at `path`: a link or a special file holds no line a scanner reads. */
+async function textAt(root: string, path: string): Promise<string> {
+  const abs = join(root, ...path.split('/'));
+  try {
+    if (!(await lstat(abs)).isFile()) return '';
+  } catch {
+    return '';
+  }
+  return readFile(abs, 'latin1');
+}
+
+/** How many times each marker occurs in `text`, one count per line it is on. */
+function markerCounts(text: string, markers: readonly string[]): Map<string, number> {
+  const counts = new Map<string, number>();
+  for (const line of text.split(/\r?\n/)) for (const marker of markersIn(line, markers)) counts.set(marker, (counts.get(marker) ?? 0) + 1);
+  return counts;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Each declared suppression marker a changed file holds on more lines than
+ * the base's copy of it does: the diff added it. Counted per file, so a
+ * marker already on the base is not the change's, and one moved into a file
+ * that did not have it is (D-R17-04). Every changed file is read whatever
+ * its extension, since a marker suppresses a finding in any file a scanner
+ * reads.
+ */
+async function markersAdded(
+  base: string,
+  head: string,
+  changes: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly change: string }>,
+  scanners: TamperOptions['markers'],
+): Promise<TamperReport['suppressionMarkersAdded']> {
+  const added: TamperReport['suppressionMarkersAdded'] = [];
+  if (scanners.length === 0) return added;
+  for (const { path, change } of changes) {
+    if (change === 'removed') continue;
+    const [before, after] = await Promise.all([change === 'added' ? '' : textAt(base, path), textAt(head, path)]);
+    for (const { kind, markers } of scanners) {
+      const was = markerCounts(before, markers);
+      for (const [marker, count] of markerCounts(after, markers)) {
+        if (count > (was.get(marker) ?? 0)) added.push({ file: path, marker, kind });
+      }
+    }
+  }
+  return added;
+}

 export type RequestProblemCode =
   /** A required list has no entries. */
   | 'missing'
   /** A string that must have content is blank, or is not a string. */
   | 'empty'

 /** The manifest's checks, or the problems that keep it from being one. */
 export function parseManifest(value: unknown): { ok: true; checks: CheckSpec[] } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
   const { problems, report } = collector();
   const checks = typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && 'checks' in value ? value.checks : undefined;
   checkProblems(checks, report);
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };

     ok: true,
     checks: (checks as CheckSpec[]).map((check): CheckSpec => {

       }

+      if (check.kind === 'unit' || isSecuritySpec(check)) return { ...check };

       return { ...check, command: [program, ...args] };
     }),

+/** The kinds a scanner runs, whose invocation is the runtime's and whose image and rules the manifest pins (D-R17-01). */
+const SECURITY_KINDS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set<SecurityCheckKind>(['secret-scan', 'static-analysis']);
+
+function isSecuritySpec(check: CheckSpec): check is SecurityCheckSpec {
+  return SECURITY_KINDS.has(check.kind);
+}
+
+/** An image pinned by digest: a name, `@sha256:`, and 64 lowercase hex digits. A tag can be moved; a digest cannot. */
+const PINNED_IMAGE = /^[^\s@]+@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$/;
+
+/**
+ * A security check's own fields: an image pinned by digest, rules as text or
+ * null, required, and no command, since the scanner's arguments are the
+ * runtime's (D-R17-01). Whether the image is the one declared for the kind
+ * is admission's, which knows the stack's scanners.
+ */
+function securityFieldProblems(c: Partial<Record<keyof SecurityCheckSpec, unknown>>, at: string, report: Report): void {
+  if (typeof c.image !== 'string' || !PINNED_IMAGE.test(c.image)) {
+    report(`${at}.image`, 'not-pinned', `${at}.image must be an image pinned by digest, name@sha256:<64 hex>; a tag is not a pin`);
+  }
+  if (c.config !== null && typeof c.config !== 'string') {
+    report(`${at}.config`, 'not-config', `${at}.config must be the scanner's rules as text, or null for the image's own`);
+  }
+  if (c.required === false) {
+    report(`${at}.required`, 'not-required', `${at} is a ${String(c.kind)} check, and must be required: a scanner whose findings hold nothing is not a control`);
+  }
+}
+
+/**
+ * Every security check the manifest must pin and every image it may: a
+ * `secret-scan` check of the secret scanner's image, and, where the stack
+ * has a static-analysis scanner, a `static-analysis` check of its image,
+ * each refused naming the kind (I5). A check pins no rules only where its
+ * scanner has rules of its own.
+ */
+export function securityProblems(
+  checks: readonly CheckSpec[],
+  scanners: { readonly secretScan: SecurityScanner; readonly staticAnalysis: SecurityScanner | null },
+): RequestProblem[] {
+  const { problems, report } = collector();
+  const declared: Readonly<Record<SecurityCheckKind, SecurityScanner | null>> = {
+    'secret-scan': scanners.secretScan,
+    'static-analysis': scanners.staticAnalysis,
+  };
+  const pinned = new Set<SecurityCheckKind>();
+  checks.forEach((check, i) => {
+    if (check.kind !== 'secret-scan' && check.kind !== 'static-analysis') return;
+    const at = `manifest.checks[${String(i)}]`;
+    pinned.add(check.kind);
+    const scanner = declared[check.kind];
+    if (scanner === null) {
+      report(`${at}.kind`, 'unbound', `${at} is a static-analysis check, and the base's stack has no static-analysis scanner to run it`);
+      return;
+    }
+    if (check.image !== scanner.image) {
+      report(`${at}.image`, 'not-pinned', `${at}.image is ${check.image}; a ${check.kind} check runs ${scanner.image}, the image declared for its kind`);
+    }
+    if (check.config === null && scanner.defaultConfig === null) {
+      report(`${at}.config`, 'not-config', `${at}.config is null, and the ${check.kind} scanner has no rules of its own; the check must pin them`);
+    }
+  });
+  for (const kind of ['secret-scan', 'static-analysis'] as const) {
+    if (declared[kind] !== null && !pinned.has(kind)) {
+      report('manifest.checks', 'missing', `manifest.checks pins no ${kind} check; every run is scanned for it (R17)`);
+    }
+  }
+  return problems;
+}

   // An optional check does not hold verify's gate, so it cannot show what the change did (D-R15-01).

     // A driver call's sandbox, the one writable sandbox, is the one whose meter is lost; the base run's check sandbox is read (D-A-BF-01).
     class UnreadableMeter extends DelegatingSandbox {
       private readonly writable = new Set<SandboxHandle>();
 
       override async provision(spec: SandboxSpec): Promise<SandboxHandle> {
         const handle = await this.inner.provision(spec);
         if (spec.mounts.workspace.mode === 'rw') this.writable.add(handle);
         return handle;
       }
 
       override async destroy(h: SandboxHandle): Promise<Teardown> {
         const teardown = await this.inner.destroy(h);
         if (!this.writable.has(h)) return teardown;
         throw new Error("the model relay's meter could not be read");
       }
     }
     const driver = new ChosenDriver({ narrative: 'built' });
     const vault = new CountingVault(components.vault);

     expect(totals.run).toMatchObject({ calls: 1, lost: 1, bound: 'lower' });

     const resumed = await resumeRun({ runId, components: built({ ...components, vault, driver }) });
     expect(resumed).toMatchObject({ ok: false, reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: [hello] });

   });

     // Nothing reaches the Vault once the process has stopped: every write after the call began is lost with it.
     class StopsMidCall extends DelegatingVault {
       stopped = false;
       private dead<T>(write: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
         return this.stopped ? Promise.reject(new Error('the process stopped')) : write();
       }
       override recordUsage(r: UsageRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordUsage(r));
       }
       override recordDecision(d: EnforcementDecision): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordDecision(d));
       }
       override recordTaskResult(id: RunId, r: TaskResult): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordTaskResult(id, r));
       }
       override writeEvidence(b: EvidenceBundle): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.writeEvidence(b));
       }
       override commitRunState(s: RunState, ifVersion: string): Promise<RunState> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.commitRunState(s, ifVersion));
       }
     }
     const vault = new StopsMidCall(components.vault);
     class StopsTheProcess extends ChosenDriver {
       override runTask(req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
         this.requests.push(req);
         vault.stopped = true;
         return Promise.reject(new Error('the process stopped during the call'));
       }
     }
     const killed = new StopsTheProcess({ narrative: 'built' });
     await expect(startRun(runRequest(runId, workspace, { ...components, vault, driver: killed }))).rejects.toThrow(/the process stopped/u);
     expect(killed.requests).toHaveLength(1);
 
     const state = await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     const totals = costTotals(await readUsage(components.vault, state));
     expect(totals.run).toMatchObject({ calls: 1, lost: 1, bound: 'lower' });
 
     const driver = new ChosenDriver({ narrative: 'built' });
     expect(await resumeRun({ runId, components: built({ ...components, driver }) })).toMatchObject({ ok: false, reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: [hello] });
     expect(driver.requests).toHaveLength(0);
   });
 
   test('a call whose usage the relay could not read makes its total a lower bound, counted apart from exact calls (D-P13-19)', () => {
     const base = { runId, taskId: hello, station: 'build' as const, attempt: 1, model: new ChosenDriver().resolveModel('standard'), collectedBy: 'runtime' as const };
     const exact = { kind: 'metered' as const, calls: 1, inputTokens: 1, outputTokens: 1, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0, costUsd: 0.01, exhausted: 'none' as const, refused: 0 };
     expect(costTotals([{ ...base, reading: exact }]).run).toMatchObject({ bound: 'exact', metered: { unreadable: 0 } });
     const totals = costTotals([{ ...base, reading: exact }, { ...base, attempt: 2, reading: { ...exact, exhausted: 'unreadable' } }]);
     expect(totals.run).toMatchObject({ bound: 'lower', metered: { calls: 2, unreadable: 1, exhausted: 1 } });
   });
 });

 async function create(io: CliIo, t: Target, args: string[]): Promise<ExitCode> {
   const { values, positionals } = parseArgs({
     args,
     strict: true,
     allowPositionals: true,
     options: {
       workspace: { type: 'string' },
       'base-commit': { type: 'string' },
       level: { type: 'string' },
       spec: { type: 'string', multiple: true },
       tests: { type: 'string', multiple: true },
       manifest: { type: 'string' },
       graph: { type: 'string' },
+      'security-part': { type: 'string' },
       'approve-cost': { type: 'string' },
     },
   });
   if (positionals.length > 0) throw new UsageError(`create takes no positional arguments: ${positionals.join(' ')}`);
   const body: CreateRunBody = {
     workspace: required(values.workspace, '--workspace'),
     baseCommit: required(values['base-commit'], '--base-commit'),
     requestedLevel: level(values.level),
     artifacts: {
       spec: values.spec ?? [],
       acceptanceTests: values.tests ?? [],
       verificationManifest: required(values.manifest, '--manifest'),
       taskGraph: required(values.graph, '--graph'),
+      // Optional on the wire: a spec created without it is refused at the spec exit, naming the part (R17).
+      ...(values['security-part'] === undefined ? {} : { specParts: { security: values['security-part'] } }),
     },
     approvedCostUsd: cost(values['approve-cost']),
   };
   const res = await call(io, t, 'POST', 'runs', body);
   const unapproved = costRefusal(res.body);
   if (unapproved !== undefined) {
     const { usd, calls } = unapproved.worstCase;
     io.err(`the worst-case cost of this run is $${String(usd)}, over at most ${String(calls)} driver calls`);
     const likely = likelyLine(unapproved.likely);
     if (likely !== undefined) io.err(likely);
     io.err(body.approvedCostUsd === null
       ? `nothing was started; to approve it, run the same command with --approve-cost ${String(usd)}`
       : `nothing was started; --approve-cost ${String(body.approvedCostUsd)} is not that figure`);
     return 1;
   }
   return report(io, res);
 }

diff --git a/packages/conformance/src/registry/headless.ts b/packages/conformance/src/registry/headless.ts

@@ -147,64 +147,64 @@ function isTypeOnly(statement: ts.ImportDeclaration | ts.ExportDeclaration): boo
 export async function assertApiRunsHeadless(): Promise<void> {
   assertCliImportsOnlyTypes();
   const token = randomBytes(32).toString('hex');
   const hostFile = join(packageDir('@olympus-ai/api'), 'test', 'fixtures', 'headless-host.ts');
   const host = spawn(process.execPath, [...NODE_ARGS, hostFile], {
     // stdin closed, stdout discarded: nothing on a descriptor the server could treat as a terminal.
     stdio: ['ignore', 'ignore', 'pipe', 'ipc'],
     env: { ...process.env, FACTORY_API_TOKEN: token },
     detached: false,
   });
   let hostErr = '';
   host.stderr?.on('data', (chunk: Buffer) => { hostErr += chunk.toString('utf8'); });
   const exited = new Promise<number | null>((done) => host.on('exit', (code) => { done(code); }));
   let hung: boolean;
   try {
     const ready = await new Promise<{ url: string; workspace: string }>((resolveReady, reject) => {
       const timer = setTimeout(() => { reject(new Error(`I9: the headless host never reported a URL\n${hostErr}`)); }, TIMEOUT_MS);
       host.once('message', (message) => { clearTimeout(timer); resolveReady(message as { url: string; workspace: string }); });
       void exited.then((code) => { clearTimeout(timer); reject(new Error(`I9: the headless host exited ${String(code)} before serving\n${hostErr}`)); });
     });
     const remote = ['--url', ready.url, '--token', token];
     const create = ['create', '--workspace', ready.workspace, '--base-commit', '0'.repeat(40), '--level', '1',
-      '--spec', 'spec.md', '--tests', 'acceptance.md', '--manifest', 'verify.json', '--graph', 'graph.json'];
+      '--spec', 'spec.md', '--tests', 'acceptance.md', '--manifest', 'verify.json', '--graph', 'graph.json', '--security-part', 'security.json'];
 
     // The figure is shown before anything starts, and nothing starts without it.
     const shown = await cli([...remote, ...create]);
     const figure = /worst-case cost of this run is \$([0-9.]+)/.exec(shown.stderr)?.[1];
     if (shown.code !== 1 || figure === undefined) throw new Error(`I9: create without an approved cost did not refuse with the figure\n${shown.stderr}`);
     // Read from the host, not the CLI: a refusal printed after a run was admitted or driven is not a refusal.
     const refused = await census(host);
     if (refused.vaultWrites !== 0 || refused.driverCalls !== 0) throw new Error(`I9: a create refused for its cost still wrote to the Vault or called a driver: ${JSON.stringify(refused)}`);
 
     const created = parse(await cli([...remote, ...create, '--approve-cost', figure]), 'create') as { runId: string };
     const streamed = await cli([...remote, 'events', created.runId]);
     if (streamed.code !== 0 || !streamed.stdout.includes('end {"kind":"outcome"')) throw new Error(`I9: the event stream did not end with the drive's outcome\n${streamed.stdout}${streamed.stderr}`);
 
     const settle = async (): Promise<View> => {
       for (let i = 0; i < 200; i += 1) {
         const view = parse(await cli([...remote, 'status', created.runId]), 'status') as View;
         if (!view.driving) return view;
       }
       throw new Error('I9: the run never settled');
     };
     const waiting = await settle();
     if (waiting.standing.standing !== 'awaiting-approval' || waiting.standing.key !== 'integrate:1') {
       throw new Error(`I9: expected the run to await integrate:1, it is ${JSON.stringify(waiting.standing)}`);
     }
     parse(await cli([...remote, 'approve', created.runId, 'integrate:1']), 'approve');
     const done = await settle();
     if (done.standing.standing !== 'passed') throw new Error(`I9: the run did not pass: ${JSON.stringify(done.standing)}`);
     // The positive control: the same count sees a run that did happen, so the zero above was measured.
     const ran = await census(host);
     if (ran.vaultWrites === 0 || ran.driverCalls === 0) throw new Error(`I9: the host counted nothing for a run that passed, so its count is not evidence: ${JSON.stringify(ran)}`);
   } finally {
     if (host.connected) host.disconnect();
     let timer: NodeJS.Timeout | undefined;
     const code = await Promise.race([exited, new Promise<'hung'>((done) => { timer = setTimeout(() => { done('hung'); }, TIMEOUT_MS); })]);
     clearTimeout(timer);
     if (code === 'hung') host.kill();
     hung = code === 'hung';
   }
   // Reached only if nothing above threw: a host that does not stop when its parent goes is a failure of its own.
   if (hung) throw new Error('I9: the headless host did not exit when its parent disconnected');
 }

           if (changed.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${changed.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (changed.actual === changed.expected) {
             throw new Error('I3: the tampered record reports the same hash for expected and actual, so nothing was re-read from disk');
           }
 
           // A deleted artifact is a mismatch, not an exception: the line must
           // record the violation and refuse, exactly as it does for an edit.
           await rm(spec);
           const deleted = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a deleted locked artifact');
           if (deleted.actual === changed.actual) {
             throw new Error('I3: a deleted locked artifact reports the same actual hash as an edited one; a deletion must be distinguishable');
           }
           if (deleted.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the deleted artifact's expected hash is ${deleted.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I3.lock-preserves-earlier-entries',
       title:
         'a later lock preserves every earlier entry, so a spec locked at `spec` is still verified after `test-design` locks the acceptance tests, and a re-lock cannot rebase an already-locked artifact\'s hash',
       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-preserve-', async (vault, dirs) => {
           const runId = 'i3-preserve' as RunId;
           const spec = join(dirs.artifacts, 'spec.md');
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
           await writeFile(join(dirs.artifacts, 'acceptance.test.ts'), "test('hello', () => { expect(run()).toBe(0); });\n");
 
           const first = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const lockedSpec = entryFor(first.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           // The second station locks its own artifact. Replace semantics would
           // drop the spec here, and I3 requires both locked and re-verified at
           // every transition.
           const second = await vault.lock(runId, ['acceptance.test.ts'], 'test-design');
           const preserved = entryFor(second.entries, 'spec.md');
           entryFor(second.entries, 'acceptance.test.ts');
           if (preserved.sha256 !== lockedSpec.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the second lock changed the spec's hash from ${lockedSpec.sha256} to ${preserved.sha256}`);
           }
           if (preserved.lockedBy !== 'spec') {
             throw new Error(`I3: the second lock rewrote the spec's station to ${preserved.lockedBy}; the station that locked an artifact is the audit record`);
           }
 
           // Verification after the second lock must still cover the first's entries.
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, quietly widened.\n');
           const tampered = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a spec edited after a later station locked its own artifact');
           if (tampered.path !== 'spec.md') {
             throw new Error(`I3: after the second lock, verification named ${tampered.path}; the spec locked at \`spec\` is no longer covered`);
           }
 
           // Re-locking is how a tamper would launder itself: lock the edited
           // file again and the manifest agrees with the disk.
           let rebased = true;
           try {
             await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'test-design');
           } catch {
             rebased = false;
           }
           if (rebased) throw new Error('I3: an already-locked artifact was locked again, which rebases its hash onto the edited bytes');
           const after = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a locked artifact after a refused re-lock');
           if (after.expected !== lockedSpec.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the refused re-lock still moved the manifest's hash to ${after.expected}`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I3.locked-artifact-cannot-be-substituted',
       title:
         `a locked artifact replaced by a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} to identical bytes outside the artifact root verifies as tampered ` +
         `rather than being followed and hashed clean (exercised on ${process.platform} via ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM})`,
       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-escape-', async (vault, dirs, base) => {
           const runId = 'i3-escape' as RunId;
           const inside = join(dirs.artifacts, 'sub');
           const locked = join('sub', 'spec.md');
           await mkdir(inside, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(inside, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, [locked], 'spec');
           const entry = entryFor(manifest.entries, locked);
           const before = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!before.ok) throw new Error(`I3: the artifact verified as tampered before anything was substituted (${JSON.stringify(before.tampered)})`);
 
           // Identical bytes at the far end. Nothing but the escape
           // distinguishes this from an intact file, which is the whole point:
           // a check that compares content alone cannot see this.
           const elsewhere = join(base, 'elsewhere');
           await mkdir(elsewhere, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           // No try/catch: a platform whose mechanism will not build fails here.
           if (ESCAPE_MECHANISM === 'junction') {
             await rm(inside, { recursive: true });
             await symlink(elsewhere, inside, 'junction');
           } else {
             await rm(join(inside, 'spec.md'));
             await symlink(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), join(inside, 'spec.md'));
           }
 
           // The escape is real before the Vault is asked about it, so the
           // verdict below cannot pass for the wrong reason.
           const reached = await realpath(join(dirs.artifacts, locked));
           const target = await realpath(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'));
           if (reached !== target) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} did not take effect on ${process.platform}: ${locked} still resolves to ${reached}`);
           }
 
           const escaped = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), `a locked artifact substituted via a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM}`);
           if (escaped.path !== entry.path) throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record named ${escaped.path}, expected ${entry.path}`);
           if (escaped.expected !== entry.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${escaped.expected}, not the ${entry.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (escaped.actual !== 'escaped') {
             throw new Error(
               `I3: a locked artifact that resolves outside the artifact root reported actual '${escaped.actual}'. ` +
                 "The bytes are identical, so anything but 'escaped' means the link was followed and the substitution was hashed clean.",
             );
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     TRANSITION_REVERIFIES_LOCKS,
     STATION_LOCKS_THE_ADMITTED_ARTIFACT,
     LOCKED_PATH_IN_DIFF_IS_TAMPER,
     // P7: tamper analysis over the trees a task was handed and verified in.
     CASE_SET_REDUCTION_IS_A_DELETION,

     SKIP_MARKER_IS_A_FINDING,

     COMPOSED_HOST_LOCKED_TEST_CHANGE_FAILS,
     // R15: an acceptance check that passes on the base is a gate the change could not fail.
     PASSING_ON_THE_BASE_REFUSES,
     DEPENDENCY_TREES_ARE_THE_BASES,
   ],
   pending: [
     pending({
       id: 'I3.coverage-report-is-not-writable-by-the-suite',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Changed-line coverage is read from the istanbul report a check run writes, and that run executes the '
         + "repository's own code: its tests, and whatever its package scripts do around them. So the artifact the "
         + 'coverage number is derived from is one the agent being judged can write. P8 closed the half of this that '
         + 'parsing can close — the report can no longer decide which lines count, only which of them ran, because '
         + "the denominator is now the union of the report's statements and the executable lines read from the head "
         + 'tree on the host (external review, codex-2 and gemini-2). The other half cannot be closed by reading the '
         + 'report more carefully: a hit count claiming a line ran cannot be told apart from one that did, by anyone '
         + 'who was not there when it ran. Closing it means the run that produces the report: coverage '
         + 'collected where the suite cannot rewrite it afterwards, or counted from an instrumentation the repository '
         + 'does not supply, with an assertion that a forged report does not raise the number. Until then a coverage '
         + 'figure is evidence about a suite that cooperated, not proof against one that did not. Recorded by P8 as '
         + 'D-P8-14. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing: the suite and the coverage writer share a process, so no '
         + 'location for the report decides whether its hit counts are true (D-P6-05).',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.unchanged-assertion-still-judges',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Tamper analysis reads the test files that changed and compares what they say. An assertion it reads as '
         + 'unchanged can stop judging without its file changing, or while the change reads as nothing: a helper, fixture, '
         + 'custom matcher, setup file, or type the test imports is rewritten to pass everything; the assertion is moved '
         + 'into a branch that never runs or a function never called; a script the pinned command runs imports a module '
         + 'that now does nothing. None of those is a test file, and reachability is not decidable by parsing. The '
         + "policy's protected paths can name the files a repository knows to be load-bearing, and a pinned command's "
         + 'own script is protected (D-P7-10), but no reading of the diff decides what an unchanged assertion now '
         + 'executes. Closing it means running the suite against mutants of the code under test and requiring the '
         + 'suite that passed at the base to kill the ones it killed there, with an assertion that a helper rewritten to '
         + 'return early, and an assertion made unreachable, each lower the kill rate and escalate. Raised by the P7 '
         + 'external review (codex-1, gemini-4, gemini-5) and recorded as D-P7-11. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing.',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.runner-process-loads-only-base-code',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         "A suite check runs the base's installed test framework, and nothing a task writes under node_modules is "
         + "carried (D-R15-05), but the runner's own process can still run code the change can reach. The installed "
